## assistant

You’re right. **Conditional does not yet match Intro to LR’s structure closely enough.** I built the curriculum, but used a simpler interface.

| Intro to LR | Conditional now |
|---|---|
| Integrated sequence of notes, tools, checkpoints, drills, practice, and capstones | Lesson list with a shared practice screen |
| Learning-path rail visible while working | General section navigation |
| Previous/next actions and lesson contents | Recommended next step, longer lesson pages |
| Shared workspace with reference/tool drawer | Separate reference pages |
| Distinct guided learning experiences | Repeated lesson and question template |

**The assignment lock is shared by both bootcamps.** It is a separate account-access issue.

My recommendation is to preserve the conditional content and rebuild its presentation around Intro’s workspace and integrated action path: **learn → guided activity → skill drill → review → mixed application**, with your three difficulty levels and targeted assignments retained.

I overstated how closely the first release matched Intro. It needs this structural pass.

## My request:
shouldnta havesequence o diffuculty it should allow them tthe stage again the curriculum according to where hte yare atw thehter e hteyaero conditional


## assistant

Yes. **The curriculum should start students where they are, rather than make everyone complete Beginner, then Medium, then Advanced.**

There should be two ways through it:

- **From the beginning:** a guided teaching sequence for someone new to conditionals.
- **From their current stage:** assign the exact skills and difficulty they need, skipping what they already know.

A student could need Beginner Group 3 language, Medium linking, and Advanced contraposition at the same time. Their curriculum should accommodate that uneven skill profile.

Difficulty should be selectable within each skill. The teaching sequence provides direction; it should not force every student through the same difficulty progression.

not diccfiluty levle selectbalei assignthem they can goto actian hting and tha can belvel 1andhighest level-3atdifepints urrciulum


## assistant

Understood. **You assign their starting point and activities. Students follow those assignments; they do not select a difficulty level.**

Levels 1–3 describe the demands of individual activities at different points in the curriculum. They are not three complete passes through the course.

You can assign the full curriculum from the beginning, start someone at a later stage, or assign one specific activity to address a gap. The curriculum’s order follows how conditional skills build on one another.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

selection.start);\\r\\n  if (selection.area) params.set(\'area\', selection.area);\\r\\n  params.set(\'path\', selection.scope === \'practice\' ? \'skill\' : selection.scope);\\r\\n  if (selection.assignedLevel && selection.scope !== \'course\') params.set(\'pathLevel\', selection.difficulty);\\r\\n  if (action === \'practice\' || selection.scope === \'skill\') params.set(\'unit\', unit.id);\\r\\n  const route = action === \'practice\' ? \'practice\' : action === \'guided\' ? `activities/${unit.id}` : `lessons/${unit.id}`;\\r\\n  return `${BASE}/${route}?${params}${action === \'repair\' ? \'#repair\' : \'\'}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction UnitActions({ unit, selection }: { unit: ConditionalUnit; selection: PathSelection }) {\\r\\n  return <nav className=\\"cb-unit-actions\\" aria-label={`${unit.title} actions`}>\\r\\n    {([[\'lesson\', \'1. Learn\'], [\'guided\', \'2. Guided activity\'], [\'practice\', `3. Skill drill · ${levelLabel(selection.difficulty)}`], [\'repair\', \'4. Review the move\']] as const).map(([action, label]) => <Link key={action} to={actionUrl(unit, action, selection)}>{label}</Link>)}\\r\\n  </nav>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction Shell() {\\r\\n  const { status, error, retry, progress } = useCourse();\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const location = useLocation();\\r\\n  const [referenceOpen, setReferenceOpen] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const [railOpen, setRailOpen] = useState(() => window.matchMedia ? window.matchMedia(\'(min-width: 641px)\').matches : true);\\r\\n  const currentId = location.pathname.match(/\\\\/(?:lessons|activities)\\\\/([^/]+)/)?.[1] ?? params.get(\'unit\');\\r\\n  const current = findConditionalUnit(currentId ?? \'\');\\r\\n  const selection = pathSelection(params);\\r\\n  const units = getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits, selection.start).filter(unit => (!selection.area || unit.area === selection.area) && (selection.scope !== \'skill\' || !selection.unitId || unit.id === selection.unitId));\\r\\n  const navigation = [[\'\', \'Curriculum\'], [\'/review\', \'Review attempts\'], [\'/diagnostics\', \'Your progress\'], [\'/journal\', \'Journal\'], [\'/settings\', \'Settings\']];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cb-shell\\"><aside className=\\"cb-rail\\"><Link to=\\"/classroom\\"><ArrowLeft size={14} style={{ display: \'inline\' }} /> Classroom</Link><div className=\\"cb-brand\\">Conditionals</div><p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Your assigned stage. One reasoning move at a time.</p><nav aria-label=\\"Conditional bootcamp\\" className=\\"cb-nav\\">{navigation.map(([route, label]) => <NavLink key={route} end={route === \'\'} to={route ? `${BASE}${route}${location.search}` : pathUrl(selection)}>{label}</NavLink>)}</nav><details className=\\"cb-curriculum-rail\\" open={railOpen} onToggle={event => setRailOpen(event.currentTarget.open)}><summary>Teaching sequence</summary>{areas.map(area => { const group = units.filter(unit => unit.area === area); return group.length ? <section key={area}><h3>{names[area]}</h3>{group.map(unit => <div key={unit.id}><Link aria-current={currentId === unit.id ? \'step\' : undefined} to={actionUrl(unit, \'lesson\', unitSelection(params, unit))}>{progress?.lessons[unit.id] ? <Check size={12} /> : <span>{conditionalUnits.indexOf(unit) + 1}</span>}{unit.title}</Link>{currentId === unit.id && <UnitActions unit={unit} selection={unitSelection(params, unit)} />}</div>)}</section> : null; })}</details></aside><main className=\\"cb-main\\" id=\\"conditional-main\\"><header className=\\"cb-workspace-bar\\"><span className=\\"cb-label\\">Conditional reasoning workspace</span><button onClick={() => setReferenceOpen(true)}><BookOpen size={16} /> Open reference</button><div className=\\"cb-save\\" role=\\"status\\">{status === \'saved\' ? \'Saved to your account\' : status === \'loading\' ? \'Loading your saved work...\' : status === \'saving\' ? \'Saving your changes...\' : status === \'unsaved\' ? \'Changes waiting to save\' : \'Changes are not saved\'}</div></header>{error && <div className=\\"cb-error\\" role=\\"alert\\"><p>{error}</p>{progress ? <button onClick={() => void retry()}>Retry save</button> : <button onClick={() => window.location.reload()}>Reload saved work</button>}</div>}{progress ? <Outlet /> : !error && <p>Loading your learning path...</p>}</main><Dialog open={referenceOpen} onOpenChange={setReferenceOpen}><DialogContent className=\\"mc-bootcamp conditional-bootcamp cb-reference-dialog\\" aria-describedby={undefined}><DialogTitle>{current?.title ?? \'Keep the reasoning close\'}</DialogTitle>{current ? <><ol>{current.method.map(step => <li key={step}>{step}</li>)}</ol><p>{current.commonMistake.repair}</p><div className=\\"cb-result\\">{current.workedExample.conclusion}</div></> : <p>Open a skill to keep its method and common mistake beside your work.</p>}<Link to={`${BASE}/reference${location.search}`} onClick={() => setReferenceOpen(false)}>Complete reference</Link><p><Link to={`${BASE}/indicators${location.search}`} onClick={() => setReferenceOpen(false)}>Groups 1–4 indicator reference</Link></p></DialogContent></Dialog></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction LearningPath() {\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const { progress } = useCourse();\\r\\n  const selection = pathSelection(params);\\r\\n  const units = getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits, selection.start).filter(unit => (!selection.area || unit.area === selection.area) && (selection.scope !== \'skill\' || !selection.unitId || unit.id === selection.unitId));\\r\\n  return <><PageHeader className=\\"cb-page-header\\" eyebrow=\\"Your conditional curriculum\\" title=\\"Build the reasoning from your assigned stage.\\" description=\\"Joshua assigns your starting stage and the work you need. Follow the teaching sequence, or return to the specific activity he assigned. Activity levels describe the demands of that task.\\" /><RecommendedForward selection={selection} /><p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Learn the move, try it with guidance, complete its skill drill, then review how it works. Saved reviews and checked attempts record work completed. They do not certify mastery.</p>{([\'conditional\', \'quantifier\'] as const).map(phase => {\\r\\n    const phaseUnits = units.filter(unit => unit.phase === phase);\\r\\n    if (!phaseUnits.length) return null;\\r\\n    return <section key={phase}><h2>{phase === \'conditional\' ? \'First, handle conditionals\' : \'Next, work with quantities\'}</h2>{phase === \'quantifier\' && <p>Some, most, and all follow conditional reasoning. Joshua can also assign this phase independently.</p>}<div className=\\"cb-sequence\\">{phaseUnits.map(unit => { const target = unitSelection(params, unit); return <article key={unit.id} className=\\"cb-card cb-path-step\\"><span className=\\"cb-step-number\\">{conditionalUnits.indexOf(unit) + 1}</span><div><span className=\\"cb-label\\">{names[unit.area]} · {levelLabel(target.difficulty)} activity</span><h3>{unit.title}</h3><p>{unit.goal}</p>{progress.lessons[unit.id] && <p className=\\"cb-muted\\"><Check size={14} style={{ display: \'inline\' }} /> Lesson reviewed</p>}<UnitActions unit={unit} selection={target} /></div></article>; })}</div></section>;\\r\\n  })}</>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction StageResources({ unit }: { unit: ConditionalUnit }) {\\r\\n  const resources = conditionalResources.filter(resource => unit.area === \'translation\' ? /indicator|practice worlds/i.test(resource.title) : unit.area === \'application\' ? /medium|advanced|practice worlds/i.test(resource.title) : unit.area === \'linking\' ? /introductory|practice worlds/i.test(resource.title) : false);\\r\\n  return resources.length ? <section className=\\"cb-card\\"><h2>Put this move to work</h2><p>Use the existing Activity Center and guided drills for additional application. These resources cover broader conditional work.</p>{resources.map(resource => <p key={resource.route}><Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={resource.route}>{resource.title}</Link></p>)}</section> : null;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction Lesson() {\\r\\n  const { unitId } = useParams();\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const unit = findConditionalUnit(unitId ?? \'\');\\r\\n  const { progress, update } = useCourse();\\r\\n  if (!unit) return <><h1>Lesson not found</h1><Link to={BASE}>Return to the learning path</Link></>;\\r\\n  const selection = unitSelection(params, unit);\\r\\n  const index = conditionalUnits.indexOf(unit);\\r\\n  const previous = conditionalUnits[index - 1];\\r\\n  const canGoBack = previous && (!selection.start || index > conditionalUnits.findIndex(item => item.id === selection.start));\\r\\n  return <><Link to={pathUrl(selection)}>Curriculum</Link><PageHeader className=\\"cb-page-header\\" eyebrow={`${names[unit.area]} · Lesson ${index + 1}`} title={unit.title} description={unit.goal} /><UnitActions unit={unit} selection={selection} /><nav className=\\"cb-toc\\" aria-label=\\"In this lesson\\"><a href=\\"#understand\\">Understand the move</a><a href=\\"#worked-reasoning\\">Watch the reasoning</a><a href=\\"#repair\\">Repair the mistake</a></nav>{unit.prerequisites.length > 0 && <p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Helpful first: {unit.prerequisites.map((id, n) => <span key={id}>{n > 0 && \', \'}<Link to={actionUrl(findConditionalUnit(id)!, \'lesson\', selection)}>{findConditionalUnit(id)?.title ?? id}</Link></span>)}.</p>}<section id=\\"understand\\" className=\\"cb-card\\"><h2>Understand the move</h2>{unit.teaching.map((paragraph, n) => <p key={n}>{paragraph}</p>)}{progress.settings.showMethod && <><h3>Your method</h3><ol>{unit.method.map((step, n) => <li key={n}>{step}</li>)}</ol></>}</section><section id=\\"worked-reasoning\\" className=\\"cb-card\\"><h2>Watch the reasoning</h2><h3>Given</h3><ul>{unit.workedExample.statements.map((text, n) => <li key={n}>{text}</li>)}</ul><h3>Work through it</h3><ol>{unit.workedExample.reasoning.map((text, n) => <li key={n}>{text}</li>)}</ol><div className=\\"cb-result\\"><strong>Result: </strong>{unit.workedExample.conclusion}</div></section><section id=\\"repair\\" className=\\"cb-card\\"><h2>Repair this mistake</h2><p>{unit.commonMistake.mistake}</p><div className=\\"cb-reason\\">{unit.commonMistake.repair}</div></section><div className=\\"cb-actions\\"><button onClick={() => update(prior => ({ ...prior, lessons: { ...prior.lessons, [unit.id]: new Date().toISOString() } }))}>{progress.lessons[unit.id] ? \'Lesson reviewed\' : \'Mark lesson reviewed\'}</button><Link className=\\"cb-button cb-primary\\" to={actionUrl(unit, \'guided\', selection)}>Next action: guided activity <ArrowRight size={16} /></Link>{canGoBack && <Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={actionUrl(previous, \'lesson\', { ...selection, difficulty: selection.assignedLevel ? selection.difficulty : getCurriculumDifficulty(previous) })}>Previous lesson: {previous.title}</Link>}</div><p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Reviewing a lesson records that you read it. Practice results are recorded separately.</p><StageResources unit={unit} /><RecommendedForward selection={selection} /></>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction GuidedActivity() {\\r\\n  const { unitId } = useParams();\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const unit = findConditionalUnit(unitId ?? \'\');\\r\\n  if (!unit) return <><h1>Activity not found</h1><Link to={BASE}>Return to your curriculum</Link></>;\\r\\n  const selection = unitSelection(params, unit);\\r\\n  return <><PageHeader className=\\"cb-page-header\\" eyebrow=\\"Guided reasoning activity\\" title={`${unit.title}: make the move yourself.`} description={unit.goal} /><UnitActions unit={unit} selection={selection} /><div className=\\"cb-card\\"><h2>Identify. Build. Test. Repair.</h2><p>Use the method to produce your reasoning before judging an answer. Keep the reference open when you need it.</p><QuestionRunner guidedMethod={unit.method} forwardSelection={selection} runKey={`guided:${unit.id}`} questions={[{ ...unit.guidedExample, unitId: unit.id, unitTitle: unit.title, difficulty: \'beginner\' }]} /></div><Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={actionUrl(unit, \'lesson\', selection)}><ArrowLeft size={16} /> Previous action: lesson</Link><Link className=\\"cb-button cb-primary\\" to={actionUrl(unit, \'practice\', selection)}>Next action: {levelLabel(selection.difficulty)} skill drill <ArrowRight size={16} /></Link></>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ntype PracticeQuestion = ConditionalQuestion & { unitId: string; unitTitle: string; difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty };\\r\\nfunction Practice({ simulator = false, hardLanguage = false }: { simulator?: boolean; hardLanguage?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const unitId = params.get(\'unit\') ?? undefined;\\r\\n  const unit = findConditionalUnit(unitId ?? \'\');\\r\\n  const selection = unit ? unitSelection(params, unit) : pathSelection(params, true);\\r\\n  const difficulty = hardLanguage ? \'advanced\' : selection.difficulty;\\r\\n  const area = hardLanguage ? \'translation\' : params.get(\'area\') ?? undefined;\\r\\n  const phase = params.get(\'phase\') === \'quantifier\' ? \'quantifier\' : \'conditional\';\\r\\n  const questions = getConditionalQuestions({ difficulty, ...(unitId ? { unitId } : {}), ...(area ? { area: area as ConditionalArea } : {}), ...(!unitId && !area ? { phase } : {}) });\\r\\n  return <><span className=\\"cb-label\\">{levelLabel(difficulty)} · {simulator ? \'Mixed application\' : \'Assigned skill drill\'}</span><h1>{unit ? unit.title : area ? names[area as ConditionalArea] : \'Put your conditional reasoning to work.\'}</h1><p>Write or diagram first. Then check the answer and repair your reasoning. Joshua assigns the stage and activity level. Hints and worked explanations remain available.</p>{unit && <UnitActions unit={unit} selection={selection} />}{questions.length ? <div className=\\"cb-card\\"><QuestionRunner key={`${difficulty}:${unitId ?? area ?? phase}`} runKey={`practice:${difficulty}:${unitId ?? area ?? phase}`} questions={questions} hideSkill={simulator} forwardSelection={{ ...selection, difficulty, area: area as ConditionalArea | undefined, unitId, phase }} /></div> : <div className=\\"cb-card\\"><p>This assignment does not match an available practice set. Return to your curriculum and ask Joshua to check its target.</p><Link to={pathUrl(selection)}>Return to your curriculum</Link></div>}</>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction QuestionRunner({ runKey, questions, hideSkill = false, forwardSelection, guidedMethod }: { runKey: string; questions: PracticeQuestion[]; hideSkill?: boolean; forwardSelection?: PathSelection; guidedMethod?: string[] }) {\\r\\n  const { progress, update } = useCourse();\\r\\n  const run = progress.runs[runKey];\\r\\n  const [timerNow, setTimerNow] = useState(Date.now());\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!run) update(previous => previous.runs[runKey] ? previous : { ...previous, runs: { ...previous.runs, [runKey]: newRun(questions.map(question => question.id)) } });\\r\\n  }, [run, runKey, questions, update]);\\r\\n  const replaceRun = (transform: (value: ConditionalRun) => ConditionalRun) => update(previous => ({ ...previous, runs: { ...previous.runs, [runKey]: transform(previous.runs[runKey]) } }));\\r\\n  const timerStartedAt = run?.timerStartedAt;\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!timerStartedAt) return;\\r\\n    const timer = setInterval(() => setTimerNow(Date.now()), 1000);\\r\\n    return () => clearInterval(timer);\\r\\n  }, [timerStartedAt]);\\r\\n  if (!run) return <p>Preparing your practice...</p>;\\r\\n  const question = questions.find(item => item.id === run.questionIds[run.index]);\\r\\n  if (!question && !run.finished) return <><p>The saved question is no longer in this set. Your earlier attempts remain in progress.</p><button onClick={() => replaceRun(() => newRun(questions.map(item => item.id)))}>Start the current set</button></>;\\r\\n  const attempts = progress.attempts.filter(item => item.id.startsWith(`${run.id}:`));\\r\\n  if (run.finished) {\\r\\n    const summary = attemptSummary(attempts);\\r\\n    return <><h2>You finished this set.</h2><p>{summary.correct} correct out of {summary.total} checked answers. {summary.assisted} used a hint or explanation before checking.</p><p>Revisit the reasoning, then continue with the next assigned action. Finishing a set does not certify mastery.</p><div className=\\"cb-actions\\"><Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={`${BASE}/review${pathUrl(forwardSelection ?? pathSelection(new URLSearchParams())).replace(BASE, \'\')}`}>Review your attempts</Link><button onClick={() => replaceRun(() => newRun(questions.map(item => item.id)))}><RotateCcw size={16} /> Repeat this set</button></div><RecommendedForward selection={forwardSelection} /></>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const draft = run.drafts[question.id] ?? emptyDraft();\\r\\n  const changeDraft = (patch: Partial<AnswerDraft>) => replaceRun(value => ({ ...value, drafts: { ...value.drafts, [question.id]: { ...(value.drafts[question.id] ?? emptyDraft()), ...patch } } }));\\r\\n  const checkAnswer = () => update(previous => {\\r\\n    const currentRun = previous.runs[runKey];\\r\\n    const currentDraft = currentRun.drafts[question.id] ?? emptyDraft();\\r\\n    if (currentDraft.checked || currentDraft.choice === null) return previous;\\r\\n    const attempt = { id: `${currentRun.id}:${question.id}`, questionId: question.id, unitId: question.unitId, difficulty: question.difficulty, choice: currentDraft.choice, correct: currentDraft.choice === question.answerIndex, hint: currentDraft.hint, explanationBeforeCheck: currentDraft.revealed, working: currentDraft.working, checkedAt: new Date().toISOString() };\\r\\n    return { ...previous, attempts: [...previous.attempts, attempt], runs: { ...previous.runs, [runKey]: { ...currentRun, drafts: { ...currentRun.drafts, [question.id]: { ...currentDraft, checked: true, revealed: true } } } } };\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  return <><div className=\\"cb-actions\\"><span className=\\"cb-label\\">{run.index + 1} / {run.questionIds.length} · {guidedMethod ? \'Guided activity\' : levelLabel(question.difficulty)}</span>{question.difficulty !== \'beginner\' && <><button onClick={() => { replaceRun(value => ({ ...value, timerEnabled: true })); setTimerNow(Date.now()); replaceRun(value => value.timerStartedAt ? { ...value, elapsed: elapsedSeconds(value), timerStartedAt: null } : { ...value, timerStartedAt: new Date().toISOString() }); }}><Clock size={16} /> {run.timerStartedAt ? \'Pause timing\' : \'Start optional timing\'}</button>{run.timerEnabled && <span aria-label=\\"Elapsed time\\">{Math.floor(elapsedSeconds(run, timerNow) / 60)}:{String(elapsedSeconds(run, timerNow) % 60).padStart(2, \'0\')}</span>}</>}</div>{!hideSkill && <p className=\\"cb-muted\\">{question.unitTitle}</p>}<h3>{question.prompt}</h3>{guidedMethod && <div className=\\"cb-reason\\"><h4>Build your response in these steps</h4><ol>{guidedMethod.map(step => <li key={step}>{step}</li>)}</ol><p>Identify the conditions. Draw the relationship. Test what follows, then explain why the tempting alternative fails.</p></div>}<label htmlFor={`working-${question.id}`}>Write your diagram or reasoning first. Arrows such as A → B are welcome.</label><textarea id={`working-${question.id}`} value={draft.working} onChange={event => changeDraft({ working: event.target.value })} placeholder=\\"What is given? What follows? Show the direction and each step.\\" /><p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Your written work is saved for comparison. It is not automatically graded.</p>{draft.working.trim() || draft.revealed ? <div className=\\"cb-options\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Answer choices\\">{question.options.map((option, index) => <button key={index} aria-pressed={draft.choice === index} disabled={draft.checked} onClick={() => changeDraft({ choice: index })}>{String.fromCharCode(65 + index)}. {option}</button>)}</div> : <p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Add your reasoning to see the answer choices, or open the explanation if you need help getting started.</p>}<div className=\\"cb-actions\\"><button onClick={() => changeDraft({ hint: true })}>Get a hint</button><button onClick={() => changeDraft({ revealed: true })}>Show worked explanation</button><button className=\\"cb-primary\\" disabled={draft.choice === null || draft.checked} onClick={checkAnswer}>{draft.checked ? \'Answer checked\' : \'Check answer\'}</button></div>{draft.hint && <div className=\\"cb-reason\\"><strong>Hint: </strong>{question.hint}</div>}{draft.revealed && <div className=\\"cb-result\\">{draft.checked && <h3>{draft.choice === question.answerIndex ? \'Your choice is correct.\' : \'Review the reasoning.\'}</h3>}<p><strong>Answer: </strong>{question.options[question.answerIndex]}</p><ol>{question.reasoningSteps.map((step, index) => <li key={index}>{step}</li>)}</ol><p>{question.explanation}</p><p className=\\"cb-muted\\">Compare your written work with these steps. A matching choice alone does not show whether your diagram was right.</p></div>}{draft.checked && <button className=\\"cb-primary\\" onClick={() => replaceRun(value => ({ ...value, index: value.index + 1, finished: value.index + 1 >= value.questionIds.length, elapsed: elapsedSeconds(value), timerStartedAt: value.timerStartedAt && value.index + 1 < value.questionIds.length ? new Date().toISOString() : null }))}>{run.index + 1 === run.questionIds.length ? \'Finish set\' : \'Next question\'} <ArrowRight size={16} /></button>}</>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Reference({ indicators = false }: { indicators?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const units = indicators ? conditionalUnits.filter(unit => /group [1-4]/i.test(unit.title)) : conditionalUnits;\\r\\n  return <><span className=\\"cb-label\\">Keep the reasoning close</span><h1>{indicators ? \'Four language groups. One direction to preserve.\' : \'Your conditional reference.\'}</h1><p>{indicators ? \'An indicator helps identify the relation. Preserve the complete condition and check what the sentence actually says.\' : \'Review the method, a worked example, and the mistake to avoid. Open the lesson or practice the exact skill.\'}</p>{units.map(unit => <section className=\\"cb-card\\" key={unit.id}><h2>{unit.title}</h2><p>{unit.goal}</p><ol>{unit.method.map((step, index) => <li key={index}>{step}</li>)}</ol><div className=\\"cb-reason\\"><p>{unit.workedExample.statements.join(\' \')}</p><strong>{unit.workedExample.conclusion}</strong></div><p><strong>Watch for: </strong>{unit.commonMistake.mistake}</p><p>{unit.commonMistake.repair}</p><div className=\\"cb-actions\\"><Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={actionUrl(unit, \'lesson\', unitSelection(params, unit))}>Full lesson</Link><Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={actionUrl(unit, \'practice\', unitSelection(params, unit))}>Practice this skill</Link></div></section>)}</>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Flashcards() {\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  const selection = pathSelection(params, true);\\r\\n  const area = selection.area;\\r\\n  const level = selection.difficulty;\\r\\n  const questions = getConditionalQuestions({ difficulty: level, ...(selection.unitId ? { unitId: selection.unitId } : {}), ...(area ? { area } : { phase: \'conditional\' as const }) });\\r\\n  return <><span className=\\"cb-label\\">Recall before reveal · {levelLabel(level)}</span><h1>Produce the rule before you recognize it.</h1><p>Work through your assigned recall set. Write your answer, then reveal the explanation and compare. Hints and early reveals are recorded separately from correctness.</p><div className=\\"cb-card\\"><QuestionRunner key={`${area}:${level}`} runKey={`flashcards:${selection.unitId ?? area ?? \'conditional\'}:${level}`} questions={questions} forwardSelection={selection} /></div></>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Diagnostics({ review = false }: { review?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const { savedProgress } = useCourse();\\r\\n  const progress = savedProgress;\\r\\n  if (!progress) return <p>Loading your recorded attempts...</p>;\\r\\n  const summary = attemptSummary(progress.attempts);\\r\\n  const misses = progress.attempts.filter(attempt => !attempt.correct || attempt.hint || attempt.explanationBeforeCheck);\\r\\n  return <><span className=\\"cb-label\\">Based on your saved work</span><h1>{review ? \'Return to the reasoning.\' : \'See what your practice shows.\'}</h1><p>Correctness, assistance, and lesson review tell different stories. These are your recorded attempts, not a placement score or an automatic mastery label.</p><div className=\\"cb-card cb-summary\\"><div><strong>{summary.total}</strong>Checked answers</div><div><strong>{summary.correct}</strong>Correct choices</div><div><strong>{summary.assisted}</strong>Assisted answers</div><div><strong>{Object.keys(progress.lessons).length}</strong>Lessons reviewed</div></div>{!review && <div className=\\"cb-scroll\\"><table className=\\"cb-table\\"><thead><tr><th>Skill</th><th>Checked</th><th>Correct</th><th>Assisted</th><th>Work on it</th></tr></thead><tbody>{conditionalUnits.map(unit => { const stats = attemptSummary(progress.attempts.filter(item => item.unitId === unit.id)); return <tr key={unit.id}><td>{unit.title}</td><td>{stats.total}</td><td>{stats.correct}</td><td>{stats.assisted}</td><td><Link to={practiceUrl(unit.id)}>Practice</Link></td></tr>; })}</tbody></table></div>}{review && (misses.length ? [...misses].reverse().map(attempt => {\\r\\n    const unit = findConditionalUnit(attempt.unitId);\\r\\n    const question = unit?.guidedExample.id === attempt.questionId ? unit.guidedExample : unit?.drills[attempt.difficulty].find(item => item.id === attempt.questionId);\\r\\n    return <article className=\\"cb-card\\" key={attempt.id}><span className=\\"cb-label\\">{levelLabel(attempt.difficulty)} · {attempt.correct ? \'Correct with assistance\' : \'Missed choice\'}</span><h2>{unit?.title ?? attempt.unitId}</h2>{question ? <><p>{question.prompt}</p><p><strong>Your answer: </strong>{attempt.choice === null ? \'No choice\' : question.options[attempt.choice]}</p><p><strong>Your working: </strong>{attempt.working || \'No written work saved\'}</p><div className=\\"cb-result\\"><strong>Answer: </strong>{question.options[question.answerIndex]}<ol>{question.reasoningSteps.map((step, index) => <li key={index}>{step}</li>)}</ol><p>{question.explanation}</p></div></> : <p>The authored question is unavailable. Your attempt remains recorded.</p>}<Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={practiceUrl(attempt.unitId, attempt.difficulty)}>Practice the skill again</Link></article>;\\r\\n  }) : <div className=\\"cb-card\\"><p>No missed or assisted attempts yet. Work through a guided example or a practice set to start your review record.</p><Link to={BASE}>Open the learning path</Link></div>)}</>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Journal() {\\r\\n  const { progress, update } = useCourse();\\r\\n  return <><span className=\\"cb-label\\">Make the correction reusable</span><h1>Your reasoning journal.</h1><p>Record the rule you misread, why your first move failed, and what you will check next time. Your account keeps these notes.</p><label htmlFor=\\"conditional-journal\\">My conditional notes</label><textarea id=\\"conditional-journal\\" style={{ minHeight: 360 }} value={progress.journal} onChange={event => update(previous => ({ ...previous, journal: event.target.value }))} placeholder=\\"I read... I first concluded... The rule actually requires... Next time I will check...\\" /></>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction Settings() {\\r\\n  const { progress, update } = useCourse();\\r\\n  return <><h1>Choose how you study.</h1><div className=\\"cb-card\\"><label><input type=\\"checkbox\\" checked={progress.settings.showMethod} onChange={event => update(previous => ({ ...previous, settings: { ...previous.settings, showMethod: event.target.checked } }))} /> Show the method checklist in each lesson</label><p>The reference always keeps the complete method. Each Level 2 or Level 3 run has its own optional stopwatch, with no time penalty or locked advancement.</p><Link className=\\"cb-button\\" to={`${BASE}/reference`}><BookOpen size={16} /> Open reference</Link></div></>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Fast Locator\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\r\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\r\\n- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`\\r\\n- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`\\r\\n\\r\\nIf an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Project Map\\r\\n\\r\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\r\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\r\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\r\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\r\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\r\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\r\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\r\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Stack And Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\r\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\r\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\r\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\r\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\r\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\r\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\r\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\r\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Working Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\r\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\r\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\r\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\r\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\r\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\r\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\r\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product Constraints\\r\\n\\r\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\r\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\r\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\r\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Smart Drill Note\\r\\n\\r\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow Expectations\\r\\n\\r\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\r\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\r\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\r\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\r\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\r\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\r\\n\\r\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\r\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\r\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known Gaps\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\r\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d4a9fb","wall_time_seconds":3.5214691,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2893,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-18\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\" — \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Best,\\r\\n> Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Apology first, as the opening line.** \\"I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\" The ownership sits in the first two sentences, before any explanation.\\r\\n2. **State their position plainly, as a confirmation.** \\"To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet.\\" Answer the question they actually asked. Don\'t mention the package or credits unless the situation calls for it.\\r\\n3. **One-sentence explanation, then \\"That is fixed now.\\"** No mechanics, no system names.\\r\\n4. **Hand them the next step, plus the manual fallback.** The link, \\"pick any time it shows and it will book,\\" and the offer to do it by hand: \\"If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\"\\r\\n5. **Invite the next report:** \\"If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\"\\r\\n6. **Close with \\"Once again, my apologies.\\"** then \\"Best,\\" and \\"Joshua\\".\\r\\n\\r\\nCut from the draft, do not reuse:\\r\\n- \\"Sorry for the runaround\\" as a sign-off. The apology opens the email; it is not a closing quip.\\r\\n- \\"You will get a calendar invite with the Google Meet link right after.\\" Do not narrate what the system will send; it reads as a promise about machinery.\\r\\n- \\"You have all 10 sessions\\" / \\"nothing was used from your package.\\" Leave the package out unless it is needed; the reassurance they want is that nothing is booked, not an accounting.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Situational micro-templates\\r\\n\\r\\nVerbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.\\r\\n  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don\'t worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.\\r\\n- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua\'s explicit go-ahead.\\r\\n- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**\\r\\n  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.\\r\\n- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.\\r\\n  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I\'m glad we got to talk through where you\'re at and what you\'re aiming for on test day.\\r\\n- **Inbound prospect (hasn\'t booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.\\r\\n  > Thanks for reaching out ... I\'d be happy to talk more about what you\'re working on and see if we\'d be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.\\r\\n- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.\\r\\n  > Please don\'t apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The three registers\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich voice governs which surface.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, recaps, in-drill feedback, email\\r\\n\\r\\nFully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.\\r\\n\\r\\nReference line (target tone):\\r\\n\\r\\n> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let\'s slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You\'ve got this.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it\'s genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.\\r\\n  > Missing this is expected. It\'s one of the hardest moves on the test. Let\'s slow down and see where it slipped.\\r\\n- **Accountability (student isn\'t doing the work):** gentle nudge, low pressure. Frame it as a slip, find a rhythm together. Never \\"the reps aren\'t happening,\\" never tough love.\\r\\n- **Praise (student nails it):** name the exact skill, tie it to their history, raise the bar.\\r\\n  > That\'s the cleanest you\'ve split premise from conclusion yet. Let\'s push it into the harder sets.\\r\\n- **Hard truth about the test:** state it plainly. \\"Do not expect for it to come easy. Expect to find this exhausting.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Teaching content (earnest substance, wit at the edges) -- bootcamp lessons, drill explanations\\r\\n\\r\\nThe substance stays earnest and clear (you are explaining a hard skill). The wit lives only at the edges: headers, openers, transitions. No jokes mid-explanation, nothing that costs comprehension.\\r\\n\\r\\n> Header: \\"The flaw hides in plain sight.\\"\\r\\n> Body: earnest, clear, step by step.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Brand (sharp wit) -- landing, auth, marketing\\r\\n\\r\\nConfident and clever, the way a good lawyer is clever. Wit tied to the real skill, not wordplay for its own sake. Playful is allowed, but every clever line must be earned and true. Hollow cleverness fails (the killed \\"the next card is the page in front of you\\" was clever but empty).\\r\\n\\r\\n> Most prep teaches the test. We teach the tell.\\r\\n\\r\\nBrand copy speaks as the platform (\\"we\\", \\"you\\"), not as an individual (\\"I\\", \\"Joshua\\"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates the marketing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Don\'t\\r\\n\\r\\n- No cold-testing or gotcha tone. (Ties to teach-first-calibrate-after.)\\r\\n- No condescension or empty cheerleading. Encouragement must sit on real substance.\\r\\n- No generic praise, no hollow cleverness, no hedging (\\"you might want to\\").\\r\\n- No hype/startup vocabulary.\\r\\n- No em dashes, ever.\\r\\n\\r\\nCorrections log: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]] records every student-facing wording fix and\\r\\nthe reason it was wrong. Read it before writing or editing LSAT U copy.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: teach-first-calibrate-after, LSAT U. Source samples: `raw/templates/Golden Email Template Playbook.md`, `raw/templates/email_writing_examples_11_full.txt`, and the full authored-email corpus from the Gmail takeout (944 messages, analyzed 2026-07-18; takeout at `raw/sources/private/takeout/`, git-ignored).\\r\\n"}'}]

The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Second brain (product memory)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.\\r\\n\\r\\nPath sanity check first:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\r\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\r\\n\\r\\nIf any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\nProtocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`/pickup`** (start / \\"brief me\\"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.\\r\\n- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / \\"done for today\\"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhere LSATU truth live…2338 tokens truncated…actions users cannot actually perform — no fake analytics, no dead buttons, no misleading placeholders. Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake data.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known gaps (do not invent answers)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run them with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows. Nothing runs `typecheck`, `lint`, or the tests automatically — `.claude/safe-push.sh` is the only gate, and only for typecheck.\\r\\n- `README.md` is a stub.\\r\\n- `.claude/settings.json` is set up with a permission allowlist for safe dev work (npm scripts, git reads, MCP GitHub reads) and a denylist for destructive ops. No custom `.claude/agents/`. A `SessionStart` auto-pull hook is wired in `.claude/settings.local.json` (runs `.claude/auto-pull-hook.sh`); Codex mirrors it in `.codex/hooks.json`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skill / agent / command vault\\r\\n\\r\\nThe full skill/agent/command vault lives in the brain root at `~/ai-os/skills/`, `~/ai-os/agents/`, `~/ai-os/commands/` (split into `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees, indexed by `~/ai-os/skills/INDEX.md`). It is inert storage — nothing here auto-loads. To use a vault skill in this project, symlink it into `.claude/skills/` (Codex: `.agents/skills/`). When you create a new skill/agent/command, save it to BOTH this project\'s `.claude/<kind>/` and the vault, so the vault stays the complete catalog.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 10.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c8b55a","wall_time_seconds":4.9064271,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3570,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 3570)\\nTotal output lines: 248\\n\\n# Copy corrections\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before\\r\\nwriting or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompanion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This\\r\\nsays what has actually gone wrong and why.\\r\\n\\r\\nStarted 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a\\r\\ncorrection is made.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The rules these corrections came from\\r\\n\\r\\nThree rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was\\r\\nenforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface\\r\\nadded later starts uncovered.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.\\r\\n- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`\\r\\n  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.\\r\\n- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nGuards now live with the content rather than with each component:\\r\\n`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every\\r\\nsurface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Corrections\\r\\n\\r\\n### Generic reasoning prompts → concrete numbered demonstrations (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua marked four parts of the V6 opening: \\"Read each whole idea,\\" \\"Find what the statements guarantee,\\" the abstract first sentence about information, and the paragraph combining application and linking. They named goals without showing a useful action or explanation. Replace them with numbered, separate scenarios. Show the fact and rule that prove a conclusion about one case; separately show two matching rules and their new conditional conclusion. Labels should identify actual inputs or outputs. Review the whole note for repeated umbrella instructions and make task instructions executable. This follows the teaching voice: specific, concrete, short sentences, with air between distinct ideas.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Fact and rule definitions must explain the inference (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected definitions that remained abstract and an explanation that did not clearly distinguish applying a rule from linking rules. Define the inputs with examples, then show the matching step and exact output. A case fact plus a rule proves something about that case. Two connecting rules produce another conditional rule without establishing a case exists. Adding a fact to the chain is a separate step. Explain all four fact situations and a hidden chain revealed by contraposition rather than leaving them implicit in an interactive model. Rules include general logical relationships, not only policies or obligations.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Boxed `L -> R` → bold teaching notation **L → R** (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected the recap\'s small monospace arrow inside a pale code box. It looked like implementation text and weakened the explanation. Use a real, clearly weighted arrow in ordinary teaching typography. Explain its complete conditions before shortening them. The Some, Most, and All reference also governs the progression: introduce the whole idea, develop the individual components, then compare and combine them. A visual refresh must review that sequence, not only colors and boxes.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Concept lesson\\" → the actual reasoning title (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe recap preview exposed an internal label above the student lesson. Joshua explicitly rejected it. Use a title such as \\"Facts, Rules, and What Follows\\" and headings that teach the reasoning. Keep production labels, version metadata, and design commentary in private records. Check the rendered HTML, since its shell can introduce wording absent from the Markdown source.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Recall Rephrase Colour\\" → \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\"\\r\\nThe door\'s heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line\\r\\nunder a chip reading \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\". \\"Rephrase\\" belongs to a different\\r\\nActivity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why\\r\\n2,600 passing tests never caught it.\\r\\n**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that\\r\\nit matches.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…\\" → plain instruction\\r\\nClinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read \\"with full\\r\\nintonation\\" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation\\r\\ninternal, \\"hear it naturally in your head\\", had been dropped in an earlier edit.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a word like \\"intake\\" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,\\r\\ncheck what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Three beats through the stimulus\\" → \\"Three steps\\"\\r\\n\\"Beat\\" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary\\r\\nsays students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four\\r\\nthings Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.\\r\\n**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"22 pieces to read\\" → line removed\\r\\nA **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same\\r\\npage use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so \\"22 pieces\\" read as 22\\r\\nsentences.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word\\r\\nalready means on the page before reusing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"say it back\\" → \\"tell it back\\", \\"The Read\\" → \\"The Recall\\"\\r\\nThe library door still carried both retired names. \\"The Read\\" was step four\'s\\r\\nformer name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was\\r\\nstale for months because it is a different database row from the sections.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"then a fourth\\" → \\"then a fourth step\\"\\r\\nThe noun sat three words back and the reader had to carry it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"once you have got all of it\\" → \\"once you have read all of it\\"\\r\\nAmbiguous between having read it and having remembered it, in the one clause\\r\\nthat triggers step four.\\r\\n**Lesson:** precision matters most where the sentence is an instruction.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The …570 tokens truncated…dent to guess what she is about to get. The sentence above the button\\r\\nstill names it, which is where a product name belongs: after the thing has been\\r\\nexplained, not as the explanation.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Instead\\" was the worse one. It tells the student she has just declined the\\r\\nassigned work by reading past it. She has not. \\"Or\\" offers a second path without\\r\\nnarrating a decision she never made.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Open analytics\\" and \\"Open journal\\" name a screen. A student does not want\\r\\nanalytics, she wants to know where the points are going.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe fallback paragraph lost \\"an adaptive set that follows your weakest question\\r\\ntypes\\" for \\"the question types you miss most\\": the same fact, without the product\\r\\nvocabulary.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** a button says what happens next, in the student\'s words. Feature names\\r\\nbelong in the sentence that explains them, never on the control.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Words never to use in student copy\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Never | Use instead |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| beat, beats | step |\\r\\n| chunk | piece |\\r\\n| intake | plain instruction |\\r\\n| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing\'s own name |\\r\\n| say it back | tell it back |\\r\\n| The Read | The Recall |\\r\\n| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |\\r\\n| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conditional Rephrase: name the student action, not the internal model (2026-09-15)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** “record the route,” “choose the route,” and a map presented as five rules.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** diagram the statement on paper, choose the matching diagram, and organize\\r\\nthe map into four groups. “Required means right” is a separate takeaway because it\\r\\nexplains placement; it is not a fifth group.\\r\\n\\r\\n“Route” described the system’s representation, not the student’s work. Calling five\\r\\nunlike ideas rules also flattened the teaching structure and made the layout harder to\\r\\nread.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** name the action the student can see herself doing. Do not force a teaching\\r\\nreminder into the same hierarchy as the categories it explains.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: [[teaching-voice]] · [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]] · [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session recaps: write a reusable reference, not a play-by-play (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** an opener saying the student got two questions right, followed by a summary of\\r\\nwhich answer they chose and how they explained it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** the concept the student needs tomorrow, a visual model of the reasoning, a\\r\\nshort diagnostic check, and the exact practice they need to complete.\\r\\n\\r\\nA student will not remember an isolated answer-choice exchange a day later, and the\\r\\nexchange is not useful unless it demonstrates a reusable rule. A recap should read like\\r\\na compact study note, not a record of the conversation. Name a question only when the\\r\\ndetails make the concept easier to apply. Praise belongs only when it identifies a\\r\\ndurable skill or meaningful change, not merely that an answer was correct.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** organize the recap around what the student can use next. Lead with the\\r\\nconcept, show the logic clearly, and finish with tangible practice.\\r\\n\\r\\n### A rule is not a fact (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** describing a conditional Must Be True lesson as “applying a rule to a fact”\\r\\nwithout checking whether the stimulus supplied a fact at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** a fact is an asserted true case or condition. A rule is a general conditional\\r\\nrelationship. It tells you what follows if its trigger is present, but it does not say\\r\\nthat the trigger occurred. Some stimuli contain rules only, so the work is to chain\\r\\nthem. When a fact is present, apply a matching rule or a valid contrapositive.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** label the role of each sentence before explaining the inference. Never\\r\\ninvent a fact to make a rule fire.\\r\\n\\r\\n### A post-session note is lecture material, not an email (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** a greeting, a dated recap heading, references to a question the student is\\r\\nexpected to remember, and a sign-off.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** a concept title, a clear opening distinction, numbered procedures, spaced\\r\\nexamples, and practice that uses the lesson.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** the note should remain useful when the session itself is no longer fresh.\\r\\nIt must teach from the page instead of relying on remembered context.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe concept should not arrive as an abrupt list. Begin with a short explanation of\\r\\nwhy the distinction matters, show the full method at a glance, and then teach each\\r\\npath in the same order. Use tables for comparisons and numbered headings for a\\r\\nprocedure so the student can see the structure before reading the detail.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Define the concept in Joshua\'s language (2026-09-28)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** “Some, most, and all tell you how much of a group fits a description.”\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** “Some means at least one. Most means more than half. All means every one,”\\r\\ngrounded in a concrete set of ten cookies before drawing conclusions from the words.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe cut line is vague and does not sound like Joshua teaching. It makes the reader\\r\\ndecode “fits a description” before learning any of the three words. It also risks\\r\\nblurring the difference between a count that is possible and one that is guaranteed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** open with the actual distinction or definition Joshua taught. Use a\\r\\nconcrete example to show what each statement proves, then introduce notation and\\r\\nthe inferences. Do not write an abstract umbrella sentence merely to announce the\\r\\ntopic.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reference quality does not authorize reference topics (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua requested the Some, Most, and All note as a teaching-flow and visual-quality reference. Quantity examples and exercises do not belong in the separate facts/rules note. Remove that off-topic material; retain the distinction between what a rule says and what a fact establishes. Before future recaps, read the accumulated corrections in the Post Session Recap skill\'s `references/recap-calibration.md`. Preserve source-driven scope while matching the reference\'s teaching quality.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4769e4","wall_time_seconds":5.1618462,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}]

ConditionalDifficulty> = {\\r\\n  orientation: \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'rules-facts\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'sufficient-necessary\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'truth-validity\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  negation: \'beginner\',\\r\\n  contraposition: \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'group-1\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'group-2\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'group-3\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'group-4\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'mixed-translation\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'implicit-conditionals\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'direct-linking\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'no-link\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  triggering: \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'and-or\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'de-morgan\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  domain: \'medium\',\\r\\n  biconditionals: \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'whole-rule-negation\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  mbt:  \'quantifier-meaning\': \'beginner\',\\r\\n  \'quantifier-negation\': \'medium\',\\r\\n  \'quantifier-linking\': \'advanced\',\\r\\n  \'quantifier-invalid\': \'advanced\',\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function getCurriculumDifficulty(unit: ConditionalUnit | string): ConditionalDifficulty {\\r\\n  return curriculumActivityDifficulties[typeof unit === \'string\' ? unit : unit.id] ?? \'beginner\';\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Starting later changes the assigned path without claiming earlier work was completed. */\\r\\nexport function getCurriculumUnits(units: ConditionalUnit[], startingUnitId?: string): ConditionalUnit[] {\\r\\n  const ordered = [...units.filter(unit => unit.phase === \'conditional\'), ...units.filter(unit => unit.phase === \'quantifier\')];\\r\\n  const startIndex = startingUnitId ? ordered.findIndex(unit => unit.id === startingUnitId) : 0;\\r\\n  return ordered.slice(Math.max(0, startIndex));\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

{'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"839873","wall_time_seconds":6.8919477,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":569,"output":"/**\\r\\n * Bootcamp Runner — the bootcamp\'s primary entry point.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Replaces the old card-grid module index. Lands the student on a single,\\r\\n * obvious next training step computed from real progress (useModuleProgress).\\r\\n * The 6-module list is demoted to a JumpTo pill row.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nimport lessonsData from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/data/lessons.generated.json\';\\r\\nimport { useModuleProgress } from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/hooks/useModuleProgress\';\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  nextTrainingStep,\\r\\n  type LessonRow,\\r\\n  type ProgressLike,\\r\\n} from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/lib/runner\';\\r\\nimport { RunnerHero } from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/components/training-runner/RunnerHero\';\\r\\nimport { Sparkles, BookOpen, Dumbbell, Target } from \'lucide-react\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst LESSONS = (lessonsData as LessonRow[]).filter((lesson) => lesson.number !== \'1.13\');\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ModuleIndex(): JSX.Element {\\r\\n  const { progress } = useModuleProgress();\\r\\n  const progressLike: ProgressLike | null = progress\\r\\n    ? {\\r\\n        completed_lessons: progress.completed_lessons,\\r\\n        completed_drills: progress.completed_drills,\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    : null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const step = nextTrainingStep(progressLike, LESSONS);\\r\\n  const isFreshRun = step.kind === \'start\';\\r\\n\\r\\n  const Icon =\\r\\n    step.kind === \'unlock-drill\'\\r\\n      ? Dumbbell\\r\\n      : step.kind === \'simulator-ready\'\\r\\n      ? Target\\r\\n      : step.kind === \'continue-lesson\'\\r\\n      ? BookOpen\\r\\n      : Sparkles;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const heroStep = isFreshRun\\r\\n    ? {\\r\\n        ...step,\\r\\n        eyebrow: \'START HERE\',\\r\\n        title: \'The Three Parts of an LR Question\',\\r\\n        subtitle:\\r\\n          \'Start by seeing the stimulus, stem, and answer choices in the right order, and what each one is asking you to do.\',\\r\\n        cta: \'Begin Lesson\',\\r\\n        // Force absolute path for navigation\\r\\n        href: \'/bootcamp/intro-to-lr/lessons/notes/three-parts-lr-question\',\\r\\n        secondary: undefined,\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    : {\\r\\n        ...step,\\r\\n        secondary: step.secondary ?? { label: \'View lessons\', href: \'/bootcamp/intro-to-lr/lessons\' },\\r\\n      };\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"px-6 py-12 desktop:px-12 desktop:py-16 max-w-[840px] mx-auto\\">\\r\\n      <section>\\r\\n        <RunnerHero step={heroStep} Icon={Icon} />\\r\\n      </section>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]
